href="/wiki/Madeira" title="Madeira">Madeira</a>, Portugal</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><div style="display:inline;white-space:nowrap;"><div    <div style="line-height:0;margin-bottom:-2px;">&#8203;</div>&#32;<div style="display:inline-block;margin-bottom:1px;">&#8203;</div>&#40;<abbr title="married">m.</abbr>&#160;<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1038841319">.mw-parser-output .tooltip-dotted{border-bottom:1px dotted;cursor:help}</style><span class="rt-commentedText tooltip" title="21 October 1911">1911</span>&#41;<wbr />&#8203;</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Issue</th><td class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1126788409">.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul{line-height:inherit;list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0}.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol li,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul li{margin-bottom:0}</style><div class="plainlist">

</div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox" style="border-collapse:collapse; border-spacing:0px; border:none; width:100%; margin:0px; font-size:100%; clear:none; float:none"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="text-align:left">Names</th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data nickname" style="text-align:left; padding-left:0.7em;">Karl Franz Josef Ludwig Hubert Georg Otto Maria</td></tr></tbody></table></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Dynasty" title="Dynasty">House</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/House_of_Habsburg-Lorraine" title="House of Habsburg-Lorraine">Habsburg-Lorraine</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Father</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Archduke_Otto_Franz_of_Austria" class="mw-redirect" title="Archduke Otto Franz of Austria">Archduke Otto Franz of Austria</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Mother</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Princess_Maria_Josepha_of_Saxony_(1867%E2%80%931944)" title="Princess Maria Josepha of Saxony (1867–1944)">Princess Maria Josepha of Saxony</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religion</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism">Roman Catholicism</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Signature</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/File:Podpis_Karla_I.png" class="image" title="Charles I&#39;s signature"><img alt="Charles I&#39;s signature" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Podpis_Karla_I.png/125px-Podpis_Karla_I.png" decoding="async" width="125" height="105" class="notpageimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Podpis_Karla_I.png/188px-Podpis_Karla_I.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Podpis_Karla_I.png/250px-Podpis_Karla_I.png 2x" data-file-width="331" data-file-height="279" /></a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Charles I</b> or <b>Karl I</b> (<a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a>: <i lang="de">Karl Franz Josef Ludwig Hubert Georg Otto Maria</i>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">Károly Ferenc József Lajos Hubert György Ottó Mária</i>; 17 August 1887&#160;&#8211;&#32;1 April 1922) was <a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_Austria" title="Emperor of Austria">Emperor of Austria</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a> (as <b>Charles IV</b>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">IV. Károly</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia-Slavonia" title="Kingdom of Croatia-Slavonia">King of Croatia</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Bohemia" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Bohemia">King of Bohemia</a> (as <b>Charles III</b>, <a href="/wiki/Czech_language" title="Czech language">Czech</a>: <i lang="cs">Karel III.</i>), and the last of the monarchs belonging to the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Habsburg-Lorraine" title="House of Habsburg-Lorraine">House of Habsburg-Lorraine</a> to rule over <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>. The son of <a href="/wiki/Archduke_Otto_of_Austria_(1865%E2%80%931906)" title="Archduke Otto of Austria (1865–1906)">Archduke Otto of Austria</a> and <a href="/wiki/Princess_Maria_Josepha_of_Saxony_(1867%E2%80%931944)" title="Princess Maria Josepha of Saxony (1867–1944)">Princess Maria Josepha of Saxony</a>, Charles became <a href="/wiki/Heir_presumptive" title="Heir presumptive">heir presumptive</a> of <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Emperor Franz Joseph</a> when his uncle <a href="/wiki/Archduke_Franz_Ferdinand_of_Austria" title="Archduke Franz Ferdinand of Austria">Archduke Franz Ferdinand of Austria</a> was <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Archduke_Franz_Ferdinand" title="Assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand">assassinated in 1914</a>. In 1911, he married Princess <a href="/wiki/Zita_of_Bourbon-Parma" title="Zita of Bourbon-Parma">Zita of Bourbon-Parma</a>. He is <a href="/wiki/Veneration" title="Veneration">venerated</a> in the Catholic Church, having been <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> on 3 October 2004, and is known to the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> as <b>Blessed Karl of Austria</b>.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Charles succeeded to the thrones in November 1916 following the death of his grand-uncle, Franz Joseph. He began secret negotiations with the Allies, hoping to peacefully end the <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">First World War</a> but was unsuccessful. Despite Charles's efforts to preserve the empire by returning it to <a href="/wiki/Federalism" title="Federalism">federalism</a> and by championing <a href="/wiki/Austro-Slavism" title="Austro-Slavism">Austro-Slavism</a>, Austria-Hungary hurtled into disintegration: <a href="/wiki/Czechoslovakia" title="Czechoslovakia">Czechoslovakia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs" title="State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs">State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs</a> were proclaimed, and <a href="/wiki/First_Hungarian_Republic" title="First Hungarian Republic">Hungary</a> broke monarchic ties to Austria by the end of October 1918. Following the <a href="/wiki/Armistice_of_11_November_1918" title="Armistice of 11 November 1918">Armistice of 11 November 1918</a>, Charles "renounced participation" in state affairs, but did not abdicate. The <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_German-Austria" title="Republic of German-Austria">Republic of German-Austria</a> was proclaimed the following day, and in April 1919 the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Parliament" title="Austrian Parliament">Austrian Parliament</a> formally dethroned the Habsburgs and banished Charles from the German-Austrian Republic for life.
</p><p>Charles spent the early part of his exile in <a href="/wiki/Switzerland" title="Switzerland">Switzerland</a>. He spent the remaining years of his life attempting to restore the monarchy. He made <a href="/wiki/Charles_IV_of_Hungary%27s_attempts_to_retake_the_throne" title="Charles IV of Hungary&#39;s attempts to retake the throne">two attempts to reclaim the Hungarian throne</a> in 1921; but failed due to the opposition of Hungary's <a href="/wiki/Calvinism" title="Calvinism">Calvinist</a> <a href="/wiki/Regent_of_Hungary" title="Regent of Hungary">regent</a> Admiral <a href="/wiki/Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy" title="Miklós Horthy">Miklós Horthy</a>. Charles was exiled for a second time to the Portuguese island of <a href="/wiki/Madeira" title="Madeira">Madeira</a>, where he soon fell ill and died of <a href="/wiki/Respiratory_failure" title="Respiratory failure">respiratory failure</a> in 1922.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Boy wearing a sailor suit" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg/170px-Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="279" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg/255px-Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg/340px-Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg 2x" data-file-width="398" data-file-height="654" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kaiser_Karl_I_as_a_child.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Charles as a child, c. 1889</div></div></div>
<p>Charles was born on 17 August 1887, in the Castle of <a href="/wiki/Persenbeug-Gottsdorf" title="Persenbeug-Gottsdorf">Persenbeug</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Lower_Austria" title="Lower Austria">Lower Austria</a>. His parents were <a href="/wiki/Archduke_Otto_Franz_of_Austria" class="mw-redirect" title="Archduke Otto Franz of Austria">Archduke Otto Franz of Austria</a> and <a href="/wiki/Princess_Maria_Josepha_of_Saxony_(1867%E2%80%931944)" title="Princess Maria Josepha of Saxony (1867–1944)">Princess Maria Josepha of Saxony</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> At the time, his grand-uncle <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Franz Joseph</a> reigned as <a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_Austria" title="Emperor of Austria">Emperor of Austria</a> and <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a>. Upon the death of <a href="/wiki/Rudolf,_Crown_Prince_of_Austria" title="Rudolf, Crown Prince of Austria">Crown Prince Rudolph</a> in 1889, the Emperor's brother, <a href="/wiki/Archduke_Karl_Ludwig_of_Austria" title="Archduke Karl Ludwig of Austria">Archduke Karl Ludwig</a>, Charles’ grandfather, was next in line to the Austro-Hungarian throne. However, his death in 1896 from typhoid made his eldest son, Archduke Franz Ferdinand, the new <a href="/wiki/Heir_presumptive" title="Heir presumptive">heir presumptive</a>.
</p><p>Archduke Charles was reared a devout <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a>. He spent his early years wherever his father's regiment happened to be stationed; later on, he lived in <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a> and <a href="/wiki/Reichenau_an_der_Rax" title="Reichenau an der Rax">Reichenau an der Rax</a>. He was privately educated, but, contrary to the custom ruling in the imperial family, he attended a public <a href="/wiki/Gymnasium_(school)" title="Gymnasium (school)">gymnasium</a> (the <a href="/wiki/Schottengymnasium" title="Schottengymnasium">Schottengymnasium</a>) for the sake of demonstrations in scientific subjects. On the conclusion of his studies at the gymnasium, he entered the army, spending the years from 1906 to 1908 as an officer chiefly in <a href="/wiki/Prague" title="Prague">Prague</a>, where he studied <a href="/wiki/Law" title="Law">Law</a> and <a href="/wiki/Political_Science" class="mw-redirect" title="Political Science">Political Science</a> concurrently with his military duties.<sup id="cite_ref-eb_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eb-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1907, he was declared of age, and Prince Zdenko von <a href="/wiki/Lobkowicz" class="mw-redirect" title="Lobkowicz">Lobkowitz</a> was appointed as his chamberlain. During the next few years, he carried out his military duties in various <a href="/wiki/Bohemia" title="Bohemia">Bohemian</a> garrison towns. Charles's relations with his grand-uncle were not intimate, and those with his uncle Franz Ferdinand were not cordial, with the differences between their wives increasing the existing tension between them. For these reasons, Charles, up to the time of the assassination of his uncle in 1914, obtained no insight into affairs of state, but led the life of a prince not destined for a high political position.<sup id="cite_ref-eb_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eb-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage">Marriage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Marriage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><audio id="mwe_player_0" controls="" preload="none" class="thumbimage" width="220" style="width:220px;" data-durationhint="76" data-mwtitle="The_Voice_of_Kaiser_Karl_I_of_Austria_-_Recording_in_1915.ogg" data-mwprovider="wikimediacommons"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/a/a5/The_Voice_of_Kaiser_Karl_I_of_Austria_-_Recording_in_1915.ogg/The_Voice_of_Kaiser_Karl_I_of_Austria_-_Recording_in_1915.ogg.mp3" type="audio/mpeg" data-title="MP3" data-shorttitle="MP3" data-transcodekey="mp3" data-width="0" data-height="0" data-bandwidth="135656" /><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a5/The_Voice_of_Kaiser_Karl_I_of_Austria_-_Recording_in_1915.ogg" type="audio/ogg; codecs=&quot;vorbis&quot;" data-title="Original Ogg file (200 kbps)" data-shorttitle="Ogg source" data-width="0" data-height="0" data-bandwidth="199972" /></audio>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Voice_of_Kaiser_Karl_I_of_Austria_-_Recording_in_1915.ogg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The voice of Emperor Charles I of Austria (1915)</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:262px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg/260px-Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="174" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg/390px-Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg/520px-Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2003" data-file-height="1340" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hochzeit_Erzh_Karl_und_Zita_Schwarzau_1911c.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Charles and Zita's wedding, <span class="nowrap">21 October 1911</span></div></div></div>
<p>In 1911, Charles married Princess <a href="/wiki/Zita_of_Bourbon-Parma" title="Zita of Bourbon-Parma">Zita of Bourbon-Parma</a>. They had met as children but did not see one another for almost ten years, as each pursued their education. In 1909, his <a href="/wiki/Dragoon" title="Dragoon">dragoon</a> regiment was stationed at <a href="/wiki/Brand%C3%BDs_nad_Labem-Star%C3%A1_Boleslav" title="Brandýs nad Labem-Stará Boleslav">Brandýs nad Labem</a> in Bohemia, from where he visited his aunt at <a href="/wiki/Franti%C5%A1kovy_L%C3%A1zn%C4%9B" title="Františkovy Lázně">Franzensbad</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Beeche_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Beeche-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 5">&#58;&#8202;5&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>It was during one of these visits that Charles and Zita became reacquainted.<sup id="cite_ref-Beeche_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Beeche-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 5">&#58;&#8202;5&#8202;</span></sup> Due to Franz Ferdinand's <a href="/wiki/Morganatic_marriage" title="Morganatic marriage">morganatic marriage</a> in 1900, his children were excluded from the succession. As a result, the Emperor pressured Charles to marry. Zita not only shared Charles's devout Catholicism, but also an impeccable royal lineage.<sup id="cite_ref-Brook-Shepherd_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brook-Shepherd-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 16">&#58;&#8202;16&#8202;</span></sup> Zita later recalled:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>We were of course glad to meet again and became close friends. On my side feelings developed gradually over the next two years. He seemed to have made his mind up much more quickly, however, and became even more keen when, in the autumn of 1910, rumours spread about that I had got engaged to a distant Spanish relative, <a href="/wiki/Jaime,_Duke_of_Madrid" class="mw-redirect" title="Jaime, Duke of Madrid">Jaime, Duke of Madrid</a>.
On hearing this, the Archduke came down post haste from his regiment at Brandeis and sought out his [step]grandmother, <a href="/wiki/Infanta_Maria_Theresa_of_Portugal" title="Infanta Maria Theresa of Portugal">Archduchess Maria Theresa</a>, who was also my aunt and the natural confidante in such matters. He asked if the rumor was true and when told it was not, he replied, "Well, I had better hurry in any case or she will get engaged to someone else."<sup id="cite_ref-Beeche_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Beeche-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 8">&#58;&#8202;8&#8202;</span></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Archduke Charles traveled to Villa Pianore, the Italian winter residence of Zita's parents, and asked for her hand; on 13 June 1911, their engagement was announced at the Austrian court.<sup id="cite_ref-Beeche_5-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Beeche-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 8">&#58;&#8202;8&#8202;</span></sup> Charles and Zita were married at the Bourbon-Parma castle of Schwarzau in Austria on 21 October 1911. Charles's grand-uncle, the 81-year-old Emperor <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Franz Joseph</a>, attended the wedding. He was relieved to see the second-in-line to the throne in a suitable marriage, and was in good spirits, even leading the toast at the wedding breakfast.<sup id="cite_ref-Brook-Shepherd_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brook-Shepherd-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 19">&#58;&#8202;19&#8202;</span></sup> Archduchess Zita soon conceived a son, and <a href="/wiki/Otto_von_Habsburg" title="Otto von Habsburg">Otto</a> was born 20 November 1912. Seven more children followed in the next decade.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Heir_presumptive">Heir presumptive</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Heir presumptive">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Charles (his father having died in 1906) became heir presumptive after the <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Archduke_Franz_Ferdinand" title="Assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand">assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand</a> (his uncle) in <a href="/wiki/Sarajevo" title="Sarajevo">Sarajevo</a> in 1914, the event which precipitated <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>. Only at this time did the old Emperor take steps to initiate the heir-presumptive to his crown in affairs of state. But the outbreak of World War I interfered with this political education. Charles spent his time during the first phase of the war at headquarters at <a href="/wiki/Cieszyn" title="Cieszyn">Teschen</a>, but exercised no military influence.<sup id="cite_ref-eb_4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eb-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Charles then became a <i><a href="/wiki/List_of_Austrian_field_marshals" title="List of Austrian field marshals">Feldmarschall</a></i> (<a href="/wiki/Field_Marshal" class="mw-redirect" title="Field Marshal">Field Marshal</a>) in the <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Army" title="Austro-Hungarian Army">Austro-Hungarian Army</a>. In the spring of 1916, in connection with the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Asiago" title="Battle of Asiago">offensive against Italy</a>, he was entrusted with the command of the XX. Corps, whose affections the heir-presumptive to the throne won by his affability and friendliness. The offensive, after a successful start, soon came to a standstill. Shortly afterwards, Charles went to the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Front_(World_War_I)" title="Eastern Front (World War I)">eastern front</a> as commander of an army<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Avoid_weasel_words" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:Avoid weasel words"><span title="The material near this tag possibly uses too vague attribution or weasel words. (September 2022)">which?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> operating against the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russians</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Romania" title="Kingdom of Romania">Romanians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-eb_4-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eb-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Reign">Reign</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Reign">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Karloath.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Karloath.jpg/220px-Karloath.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Karloath.jpg/330px-Karloath.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Karloath.jpg/440px-Karloath.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="786" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Karloath.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>King Charles IV taking his <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_the_Hungarian_monarch" title="Coronation of the Hungarian monarch">coronation oath</a> at Holy Trinity Column outside <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Church" title="Matthias Church">Matthias Church</a>, <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a>, 30 December 1916</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Karlfamily.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/5/5a/Karlfamily.jpg/220px-Karlfamily.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/5/5a/Karlfamily.jpg/330px-Karlfamily.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/5/5a/Karlfamily.jpg/440px-Karlfamily.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="547" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Karlfamily.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Portrait of the recently crowned King Charles IV of Hungary and <a href="/wiki/Zita_of_Bourbon-Parma" title="Zita of Bourbon-Parma">Queen Zita</a> with their son <a href="/wiki/Otto_von_Habsburg" title="Otto von Habsburg">Otto</a></div></div></div>
<p>Charles succeeded to the thrones on 21 November 1916 upon the death of his grand-uncle, Emperor Franz Joseph. On 2 December 1916, he assumed the title of Supreme Commander of the <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Army" title="Austro-Hungarian Army">Austro-Hungarian Army</a>, succeeding <a href="/wiki/Archduke_Friedrich,_Duke_of_Teschen" title="Archduke Friedrich, Duke of Teschen">Archduke Friedrich</a>. His coronation as King of Hungary occurred on 30 December. In 1917, Charles secretly entered into peace negotiations with the <a href="/wiki/Third_French_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Third French Republic">Third French Republic</a>. He employed his brother-in-law, <a href="/wiki/Prince_Sixtus_of_Bourbon-Parma" title="Prince Sixtus of Bourbon-Parma">Prince Sixtus of Bourbon-Parma</a>, an officer in the <a href="/wiki/Belgian_Army" class="mw-redirect" title="Belgian Army">Belgian Army</a>, as intermediary. However, the Allies insisted on Austrian recognition of Italian claims to territory and Charles refused, so no progress was made.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Foreign minister <a href="/wiki/Ottokar_Czernin" title="Ottokar Czernin">Graf Czernin</a> was only interested in negotiating a general peace which would include Germany; Charles himself went much further in suggesting his willingness to make a separate peace. When news of the overture leaked in April 1918, Charles denied involvement until French Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Georges_Clemenceau" title="Georges Clemenceau">Georges Clemenceau</a> published letters signed by him. This led to Czernin's resignation, forcing Austria-Hungary to give Berlin full control of its armed forces, factories, and railways.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Austro-Hungarian Empire was wracked by inner turmoil in the final years of the war, with escalating tension between ethnic groups. As part of his <a href="/wiki/Fourteen_Points" title="Fourteen Points">Fourteen Points</a>, U.S. President <a href="/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson" title="Woodrow Wilson">Woodrow Wilson</a> demanded that the Empire allow for autonomy and self-determination of its peoples. In response, Charles agreed to reconvene the <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Council_(Austria)" title="Imperial Council (Austria)">Imperial Parliament</a> and allow for the creation of a confederation with each national group exercising self-governance. However, the ethnic groups fought for full autonomy as separate nations, as they were now determined to become independent from Vienna at the earliest possible moment.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The new foreign minister Baron <a href="/wiki/Stephan_Buri%C3%A1n_von_Rajecz" title="Stephan Burián von Rajecz">Istvan Burián</a> asked for an armistice on 14 October based on the Fourteen Points, and two days later Charles issued a proclamation that radically changed the nature of the Austrian state. The Poles were granted full independence with the purpose of joining their ethnic brethren in Russia and Germany in what was to become the <a href="/wiki/Second_Polish_Republic" title="Second Polish Republic">Second Polish Republic</a>. The rest of the Austrian lands were transformed into a federal union composed of four parts: German, Czech, South Slav, and Ukrainian. Each of the four parts was to be governed by a federal council, and <a href="/wiki/Trieste" title="Trieste">Trieste</a> was to have a special status. However, U.S. Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Robert_Lansing" title="Robert Lansing">Robert Lansing</a> replied four days later that the Allies were committed to the political independence of the Czechs, Slovaks and South Slavs, and that autonomy inside the Empire was no longer enough. In fact, a <a href="/wiki/Czechoslovakia" title="Czechoslovakia">Czechoslovak provisional government</a> had joined the Allies on 14 October, and the South Slav national council declared an <a href="/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs" title="State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs">independent South Slav state</a> on 29 October 1918.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Trialism_and_Croatia">Trialism and Croatia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Trialism and Croatia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>From the beginning of his reign, Charles l favored the creation of a third Croatian political entity in the Empire, in addition to Austria and Hungary. In his Croatian coronation oath in 1916, he recognized the union of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia-Slavonia" title="Kingdom of Croatia-Slavonia">Triune Kingdom of Croatia, Dalmatia and Slavonia</a> with <a href="/wiki/Corpus_separatum_(Fiume)" title="Corpus separatum (Fiume)">Rijeka</a><sup id="cite_ref-Karlova_zavjernica_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Karlova_zavjernica-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> and during his short reign supported <a href="/wiki/Trialism_in_Austria-Hungary" title="Trialism in Austria-Hungary">trialist</a> suggestions from the <a href="/wiki/Croatian_Parliament" title="Croatian Parliament">Croatian Sabor</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ban_of_Croatia" title="Ban of Croatia">Ban</a>; however, the suggestions were always vetoed by the <a href="/wiki/Diet_of_Hungary" title="Diet of Hungary">Hungarian Parliament</a>, which did not want to share power with other nations. After Emperor Charles's manifesto of 14 October 1918 was rejected by the declaration of the <a href="/wiki/National_Council_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs" class="mw-redirect" title="National Council of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs">National Council</a> in Zagreb.<sup id="cite_ref-F._Šišić_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-F._Šišić-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> President of the Croatian pro-monarchy political party <a href="/wiki/Pure_Party_of_Rights" class="mw-redirect" title="Pure Party of Rights">Pure Party of Rights</a> Dr. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Aleksandar_Horvat&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Aleksandar Horvat (page does not exist)">Aleksandar Horvat</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://hr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar_Horvat" class="extiw" title="hr:Aleksandar Horvat">hr</a>&#93;</span>, with other parliament members and generals went to visit the emperor on 21 October 1918 in Bad Ischl,<sup id="cite_ref-Kazimirović_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kazimirović-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Jedna_infamija_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jedna_infamija-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> where the emperor agreed and signed the trialist manifesto under the proposed terms set by the delegation, on the condition that the Hungarian part does the same since he swore an oath on the integrity of the Hungarian crown.<sup id="cite_ref-A._PAVELIĆ_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-A._PAVELIĆ-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Dr._Aleksandar_Horvat_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dr._Aleksandar_Horvat-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Edmund_von_Glaise-Horstenau_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edmund_von_Glaise-Horstenau-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The delegation went the next day to Budapest where it presented the manifesto to Hungarian officials and Council of Ministers who signed the manifesto and released the king from his oath, creating a third Croatian political entity (Zvonimir's kingdom).<sup id="cite_ref-Dr._Aleksandar_Horvat_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dr._Aleksandar_Horvat-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Budisavljević_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Budisavljević-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-F._Milobar_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-F._Milobar-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-S._MATKOVIĆ_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-S._MATKOVIĆ-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> After the signing, two parades were held in Zagreb, one for the ending of the <a href="/wiki/K.u.K." class="mw-redirect" title="K.u.K.">K.u.K.</a> monarchy, which was held in front of the Croatian National Theater, and another one for saving the trialist monarchy.<sup id="cite_ref-Budisavljević_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Budisavljević-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The last vote for the support of the trialist reorganization of the empire was, however, too late. On 29 October 1918, the Croatian Sabor (parliament) ended the union and all ties with Hungary and Austria, proclaimed the unification of all Croatian lands and entered the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs" title="State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs">State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Hrvatska_Država_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hrvatska_Država-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The curiosity is that no act of Sabor dethroned King Charles IV, nor did it acknowledge the entering in a state union with Serbia, which is today mentioned in the preamble of the Constitution of Croatia.<sup id="cite_ref-Narodne_novine_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Narodne_novine-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Lansing note effectively ended any efforts to keep the Empire together. One by one, the nationalities proclaimed their independence; even before the note the national councils had been acting more like provisional governments. Charles's political future became uncertain.  On 31 October, Hungary officially ended the <a href="/wiki/Personal_union" title="Personal union">personal union</a> between Austria and Hungary. Nothing remained of Charles's realm except the predominantly German-speaking Danubian and Alpine provinces, and he was challenged even there by the <a href="/wiki/German_Austria" class="mw-redirect" title="German Austria">German Austrian</a> State Council. His last Austrian prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Heinrich_Lammasch" title="Heinrich Lammasch">Heinrich Lammasch</a>, advised him that he was in an impossible situation, and his best course was to temporarily give up his right to exercise sovereign power.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Proclamations_of_November_1918"><span id="Proclamations"></span>Proclamations of November 1918</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Proclamations of November 1918">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg/220px-Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="167" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg/330px-Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg/440px-Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2472" data-file-height="1872" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Verzichtserkl%C3%A4rung_Karl_I._11.11.1918.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Proclamation of 11 November 1918</div></div></div>
<p>On the day of the <a href="/wiki/Armistice_of_11_November_1918" title="Armistice of 11 November 1918">Armistice of 11 November 1918</a>, Charles issued a carefully worded proclamation in which he recognized the Austrian people's right to determine the form of the state and "relinquish[ed] every participation in the administration of the State."<sup id="cite_ref-Duffy_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Duffy-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> He also released his officials from their oath of loyalty to him. On the same day, the Imperial Family left <a href="/wiki/Sch%C3%B6nbrunn_Palace" title="Schönbrunn Palace">Schönbrunn Palace</a> and moved to <a href="https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schloss_Eckartsau" class="extiw" title="de:Schloss Eckartsau">Schloss Eckartsau</a>, east of Vienna. On 13 November, following a visit with Hungarian magnates, Charles issued a similar proclamation—the Eckartsau Proclamation—for Hungary.
</p><p>Although it has widely been cited as an "abdication", the word itself was never used in either proclamation.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Indeed, he deliberately avoided using the word <i>abdication</i> in the hope that the people of either Austria or Hungary would vote to recall him. Privately, Charles left no doubt that he believed himself to be the rightful emperor. He wrote to <a href="/wiki/Friedrich_Gustav_Piffl" title="Friedrich Gustav Piffl">Friedrich Gustav Piffl</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Vienna" title="Archbishop of Vienna">Archbishop of Vienna</a>:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>I did not abdicate, and never will&#160;[...] I see my manifesto of 11 November as the equivalent to a cheque which a street thug has forced me to issue at gunpoint&#160;[...] I do not feel bound by it in any way whatsoever."<sup id="cite_ref-Scheidl_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scheidl-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg/220px-Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="247" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg/330px-Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg/440px-Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg 2x" data-file-width="740" data-file-height="832" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Eckartsaui_nyilatkozat.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Proclamation of 13 November 1918</div></div></div>
<p>Instead, on 12 November, the day after he issued his proclamation, the independent <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_German-Austria" title="Republic of German-Austria">Republic of German-Austria</a> was proclaimed, followed by the proclamation of the <a href="/wiki/First_Hungarian_Republic" title="First Hungarian Republic">First Hungarian Republic</a> on 16 November. An uneasy truce-like situation ensued and persisted until 23 to 24 March 1919, when Charles left for Switzerland, escorted by the commander of the small British guard detachment at Eckartsau, Lieutenant Colonel <a href="/wiki/Edward_Lisle_Strutt" title="Edward Lisle Strutt">Edward Lisle Strutt</a>.
</p><p>As the imperial train left Austria on 24 March, Charles issued another proclamation in which he confirmed his claim of sovereignty, declaring that: 
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>[W]hatever the national assembly of German Austria has resolved with respect to these matters since 11 November is null and void for me and my House.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The newly established republican government of Austria was not aware of this "Manifesto of <a href="/wiki/Feldkirch,_Vorarlberg" title="Feldkirch, Vorarlberg">Feldkirch</a>" at this time—it had been dispatched only to King <a href="/wiki/Alfonso_XIII_of_Spain" class="mw-redirect" title="Alfonso XIII of Spain">Alfonso XIII of Spain</a> and to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a> through diplomatic channels—and politicians in power were irritated by the Emperor's departure without explicit abdication.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Parliament" title="Austrian Parliament">Austrian Parliament</a> responded on 3 April with the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_Law" title="Habsburg Law">Habsburg Law</a>, which dethroned and banished the Habsburgs. Charles was barred from ever returning to Austria. Other male Habsburgs could only return if they renounced all intentions of reclaiming the defunct throne and accepted the status of ordinary citizens. Another law passed on the same day abolished all nobility in Austria. In Switzerland, Charles and his family briefly took residence at Castle Wartegg near <a href="/wiki/Rorschach,_Switzerland" title="Rorschach, Switzerland">Rorschach</a> at <a href="/wiki/Lake_Constance" title="Lake Constance">Lake Constance</a>, and later moved to Château de <a href="/wiki/Prangins" title="Prangins">Prangins</a> at <a href="/wiki/Lake_Geneva" title="Lake Geneva">Lake Geneva</a> on 20 May.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Attempts_to_reclaim_throne_of_Hungary">Attempts to reclaim throne of Hungary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Attempts to reclaim throne of Hungary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Charles_IV_of_Hungary%27s_attempts_to_retake_the_throne" title="Charles IV of Hungary&#39;s attempts to retake the throne">Charles IV of Hungary's attempts to retake the throne</a></div>
<p>Following the restoration of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1920%E2%80%931946)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1920–1946)">Kingdom of Hungary</a>, encouraged by Hungarian royalists ("legitimists"), Charles sought twice in 1921 to reclaim the throne of Hungary, but failed largely because Hungary's <a href="/wiki/Regent" title="Regent">regent</a>, <a href="/wiki/Admiral" title="Admiral">Admiral</a> <a href="/wiki/Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy" title="Miklós Horthy">Miklós Horthy</a> (the last commander of the <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Navy" title="Austro-Hungarian Navy">Imperial and Royal Navy</a>), refused to support Charles's restoration. Horthy's action was declared "treasonous" by royalists. Critics suggest that Horthy's actions were more firmly grounded in political reality than those of Charles and his supporters. Indeed, <a href="/wiki/Little_Entente" title="Little Entente">neighbouring countries</a> had threatened to invade Hungary if Charles tried to regain the throne. Later in 1921, the Hungarian parliament formally nullified the <a href="/wiki/Pragmatic_Sanction_of_1713" title="Pragmatic Sanction of 1713">Pragmatic Sanction</a>, an act that effectively dethroned the Habsburgs in Hungary.
</p>
<h2><span id="Exile_in_Madeira.2C_Portugal.2C_and_death"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Exile_in_Madeira,_Portugal,_and_death">Exile in Madeira, Portugal, and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Exile in Madeira, Portugal, and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg/220px-Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg/330px-Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg/440px-Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2448" data-file-height="3264" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Karl_Grabmal_Monte.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tomb of Charles I in Madeira</div></div></div>
<p>After the second failed attempt at restoration in Hungary, Charles and his pregnant wife Zita were arrested and quarantined at <a href="/wiki/Tihany_Abbey" title="Tihany Abbey">Tihany Abbey</a>. Determined to prevent a third restoration attempt, the Council of Allied Powers decided on his exile. On 1 November 1921 they were transported down the Danube aboard the <a href="/wiki/Gunboat" title="Gunboat">gunboat</a> <a href="/wiki/Insect-class_gunboat" title="Insect-class gunboat">HMS <i>Glowworm</i></a>, across the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> on the cruiser <a href="/wiki/HMS_Cardiff_(D58)" title="HMS Cardiff (D58)">HMS&#160;<i>Cardiff</i></a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Brook_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brook-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> and on 19 November 1921 they arrived at their final exile, the isolated, heavily guarded Portuguese island of <a href="/wiki/Madeira_Island" title="Madeira Island">Madeira</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The couple and their children, who joined them on 2 February 1922, lived first at <a href="/wiki/Funchal" title="Funchal">Funchal</a> at the Villa Vittoria, next to <a href="/wiki/Belmond_Reid%27s_Palace" title="Belmond Reid&#39;s Palace">Reid's Hotel</a>, and later moved to a modest residence in Quinta do <a href="/wiki/Monte_(Funchal)" title="Monte (Funchal)">Monte</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pamphlet_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pamphlet-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Charles never left Madeira. On 9 March 1922 he caught a cold in town, which developed into bronchitis and progressed to severe <a href="/wiki/Pneumonia" title="Pneumonia">pneumonia</a>. Having suffered two heart attacks, he died of <a href="/wiki/Respiratory_failure" title="Respiratory failure">respiratory failure</a> on 1 April, in the presence of his wife (who was pregnant with their eighth child) and nine-year-old former <a href="/wiki/Otto_von_Habsburg" title="Otto von Habsburg">Crown Prince Otto</a>, remaining conscious almost until his last moments. His last words to his wife were "I love you so much."<sup id="cite_ref-dies_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dies-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> He was 34 years old. His remains except for his heart are still on the island, resting in state in a chapel devoted to the Emperor in the Portuguese <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Mount" title="Church of Our Lady of the Mount">Church of Our Lady of the Mount</a>, in spite of several attempts to move them to the <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Crypt" title="Imperial Crypt">Habsburg Crypt</a> in Vienna. His heart and the heart of his wife are entombed in <a href="/wiki/Muri_Abbey" title="Muri Abbey">Muri Abbey</a>, Switzerland.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg/220px-Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="306" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg/330px-Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg/440px-Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3812" data-file-height="5307" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_I_of_Austria.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Charles as Archduke</div></div></div>
<p>Historians have been mixed in their evaluations of Charles and his reign. In the <a href="/wiki/Interwar_period" title="Interwar period">interwar period</a>, he was celebrated in Austria as a military hero. When Nazi Germany took over it made his memory into that of a traitor. For decades after 1945, both popular and academic interest practically disappeared, but attention has slowly returned.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Helmut Rumpler, the head of the Habsburg commission of the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Academy_of_Sciences" title="Austrian Academy of Sciences">Austrian Academy of Sciences</a>, described Charles as "a dilettante, far too weak for the challenges facing him, out of his depth, and not really a politician."<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Others have seen Charles as a brave and honourable figure who tried to stop the war in which his Empire was drowning.
<a href="/wiki/Anatole_France" title="Anatole France">Anatole France</a>, the French novelist, stated: 
</p>
<blockquote><p>This war without end is criminal. What is abominable is that they do not want to end it. No, they do not want. Do not try to tell me that there was no way to end it. Emperor Charles offered peace; he is the only decent man to have appeared in this war, and he was not listened to. There was, through him, a chance that could have been seized... Clemenceau called the emperor a "rotten conscience," it's ignoble. Emperor Charles sincerely wanted peace, and therefore was despised by the whole world. [...] A king of France, yes a king, would have had pity on our poor, exhausted, bloodlet nation. However democracy is without a heart and without entrails. When serving the powers of money, it is pitiless and inhuman.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p><a href="/wiki/Paul_von_Hindenburg" title="Paul von Hindenburg">Paul von Hindenburg</a>, the German commander in chief, commented in his memoirs:</p><blockquote><p>He tried to compensate for the evaporation of the ethical power which emperor Franz Joseph had represented by offering <a href="/wiki/V%C3%B6lkisch_movement" title="Völkisch movement">völkisch</a> reconciliation. Even as he dealt with elements who were sworn to the goal of destroying his empire he believed that his acts of political grace would affect their conscience. These attempts were totally futile; those people had long ago lined up with our common enemies, and were far from being deterred.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification">Beatification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Beatification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Blessed</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Karl of Austria</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png" class="image"><img alt="IV. Károly a Szent István-rend ornátusában.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png/200px-IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png" decoding="async" width="200" height="280" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png/300px-IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png/400px-IV._K%C3%A1roly_a_Szent_Istv%C3%A1n-rend_orn%C3%A1tus%C3%A1ban.png 2x" data-file-width="626" data-file-height="877" /></a><div class="infobox-caption">Charles I of Austria with the habit of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Stephen_of_Hungary" title="Order of Saint Stephen of Hungary">Order of Saint Stephen of Hungary</a></div></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;"><a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_Austria" title="Emperor of Austria">Emperor</a>; <a href="/wiki/Confessor_of_the_Faith" title="Confessor of the Faith">Confessor</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Beatified</a></th><td class="infobox-data">3 October 2004, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Square" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Square">Saint Peter's Square</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><span class="nowrap">Major <a href="/wiki/Shrine" title="Shrine">shrine</a></span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Church_of_Our_Lady_of_Monte" class="mw-redirect" title="Church of Our Lady of Monte">Church of Our Lady of Monte</a>, <a href="/wiki/Funchal" title="Funchal">Funchal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/21_October" class="mw-redirect" title="21 October">21 October</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409"/><div class="plainlist"><ul><li>Imperial attire</li><li>Medals</li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/World_peace" title="World peace">World peace</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> leaders have praised Charles for putting his Christian faith first in making political decisions, and for his role as a peacemaker during the war, especially after 1917. 
</p><p>The <i>cause</i> or campaign for his canonization began in 1949. In 1954, the cause was opened and Charles was declared "<a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">servant of God</a>", the first step in the process. At the beginning of the cause for canonization in 1972 his tomb was opened and his body was discovered to be <a href="/wiki/Incorruptibility" title="Incorruptibility">incorrupt</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 14 April 2003, the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Vatican's</a> <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>, in the presence of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, promulgated Charles of Austria's "<a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtues</a>". Charles thereby acquired the title "<a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">venerable</a>". On 21 December 2003, the Congregation certified, on the basis of three expert medical opinions, that a miracle in 1960 occurred through the <a href="/wiki/Intercession_of_saints" title="Intercession of saints">intercession</a> of Charles. The miracle attributed to Charles was the scientifically inexplicable healing of a Brazilian <a href="/wiki/Nun" title="Nun">nun</a> with debilitating <a href="/wiki/Varicose_vein" class="mw-redirect" title="Varicose vein">varicose veins</a>; she was able to get out of bed after she prayed for his <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>
Pope John Paul II declared Charles "Blessed" in a <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> ceremony in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a> on 3 October 2004. The Pope also declared 21 October, the date of Charles's marriage in 1911 to Princess Zita, to be Charles's <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sondhaus_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sondhaus-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> At the ceremony, the Pope stated:<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The decisive task of Christians consists in seeking, recognizing and following God's will in all things. The Christian statesman, Charles of Austria, confronted this challenge every day. To his eyes, war appeared as "something appalling". Amid the tumult of the First World War, he strove to promote the peace initiative of my Predecessor, <a href="/wiki/Benedict_XV" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedict XV">Benedict XV</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The main points of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a>'s peace plan were:  (1) the moral force of right ... be substituted for the material force of arms, (2) there must be simultaneous and reciprocal diminution of armaments, (3) a mechanism for international arbitration must be established, (4) true liberty and common rights over the sea should exist, (5) there should be a renunciation of war indemnities, (6) occupied territories should be evacuated, and (7) there should be an examination of rival claims.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> The best outcome to the war, according to Pope Benedict XV, was an immediate restoration of the status quo without reparations or any form of forced demands.  Although the plan seemed unattainable due to the severity of the war thus far, it appealed to Charles, perhaps as a way to fulfill and preserve his role as Catholic Emperor of Austria and Apostolic King of Hungary.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The beatification nevertheless raised controversy over Charles's alleged authorisation of the <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Army" title="Austro-Hungarian Army">Austro-Hungarian Army</a>'s use of <a href="/wiki/Poison_gas" class="mw-redirect" title="Poison gas">poison gas</a> during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Standard_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Standard-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From the beginning, Emperor Charles conceived of his office as a holy service to his people. His chief concern was to follow the Christian vocation to holiness also in his political actions. For this reason, his thoughts turned to social assistance.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 31 January 2008, after a 16-month investigation, a Church tribunal recognized a second miracle attributed to Charles I. A "devout <a href="/wiki/Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Baptist">Baptist</a>" from <a href="/wiki/Orlando" class="mw-redirect" title="Orlando">Orlando</a>, <a href="/wiki/Florida" title="Florida">Florida</a> was allegedly cured after several recent converts to Roman Catholicism in <a href="/wiki/Louisiana" title="Louisiana">Louisiana</a> prayed for Charles's intercession.<sup id="cite_ref-Goodman_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goodman-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Pinsky_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pinsky-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2011, the League of Prayers for the promotion of Charles's cause set up a website,<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> and Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Christoph_Sch%C3%B6nborn" title="Christoph Schönborn">Christoph Schönborn</a> of <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a> has sponsored the cause.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> One of Charles's granddaughters, <a href="/wiki/Maria-Anna_Galitzine" title="Maria-Anna Galitzine">Princess Maria-Anna Galitzine</a>, has been a prominent figure in the campaign for sainthood.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Quotes">Quotes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Quotes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Copy_to_Wikiquote plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-move" <ul><li>"Now, we must help each other to get to Heaven."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogle200535_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogle200535-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Addressing Empress Zita on 22 October 1911, the day after their wedding.</li>
<li>"I am an officer with all my body and soul, but I do not see how anyone who sees his dearest relations leaving for the front can love war."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogle200554_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogle200554-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Addressing Empress Zita after the outbreak of <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>.</li>
<li>"I have done my duty, as I came here to do. As crowned King, I not only have a right, I also have a duty. I must uphold the right, the dignity and honor of the Crown.... For me, this is not something light. With the last breath of my life I must take the path of duty. Whatever I regret, Our Lord and Savior has led me."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogle2005137_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogle2005137-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Addressing Cardinal <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_Csernoch" title="János Csernoch">János Csernoch</a> after the defeat of his attempt to regain the Hungarian throne in 1921.</li>
<li>"I must suffer like this so my people will come together again."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogle2005143_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogle2005143-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Spoken in Madeira, during his last illness.</li>
<li>"I can't go on much longer... Thy will be done... Yes... Yes... As you will it... Jesus!"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogle2005144_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogle2005144-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Reciting his last words while contemplating a <a href="/wiki/Crucifix" title="Crucifix">crucifix</a> held by Empress Zita.</li></ul>
<h2><span id="Titles.2C_styles.2C_honours_and_arms"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Titles,_styles,_honours_and_arms">Titles, styles, honours and arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Titles, styles, honours and arms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: #CCBBEE; color: #000066;"><span style="font-size:80%">Styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Charles I of Austria</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><div class="center"><div class="floatnone"><a href="/wiki/File:Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg" class="image"><img alt="Imperial Coat of Arms of the Empire of Austria.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg/105px-Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg.png" decoding="async" width="105" height="129" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg/158px-Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg/210px-Imperial_Coat_of_Arms_of_the_Empire_of_Austria.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="631" /></a></div></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Majesty" title="Apostolic Majesty">His Imperial and Royal Apostolic Majesty</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Imperial and Royal Apostolic Majesty</td></tr></tbody></table>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Titles_and_styles">Titles and styles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Titles and styles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>17 August 1887 – 28 June 1914: <i>His Imperial and Royal Highness</i> Archduke Charles of Austria, Prince of Hungary, Bohemia and Croatia<sup id="cite_ref-harmonische_Wahlkapitulation_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-harmonische_Wahlkapitulation-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>28 June 1914 – 21 November 1916: <i>His Imperial and Royal Highness</i> The Archduke of Austria-Este<sup id="cite_ref-HofUndStaat_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HofUndStaat-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>21 November 1916 – 3 April 1919: <i>His Imperial and Royal Apostolic Majesty</i> The Emperor of Austria, Apostolic King of Hungary and Croatia, Slavonia and Dalmatia<sup id="cite_ref-Croatian_Coronation_Oath_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Croatian_Coronation_Oath-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Postage_stamp">Postage stamp</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Postage stamp">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>Issued by Hungary on 30 December 1916<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Children">Children</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_I_of_Austria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Children">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Charles and Princess Zita of Bourbon-Parma had eight children together.
</p>

<td><span style="display:none">(<span class="bday">1921-03-01</span>)</span>1 March 1921</td>
<td>23 July 1989<span style="display:none">(1989-07-23)</span> (aged&#160;68)</td>
<td>married (1956) <a href="/wiki/George,_Duke_of_Mecklenburg" title="George, Duke of Mecklenburg">George, Duke of Mecklenburg</a> (5 October&#160;&#91;<a href="/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" title="Old Style and New Style dates">O.S.</a> 22 September&#93;&#160;1899&#160;&#8211;&#32;6 July 1963).
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>Archduchess <b><a href="/wiki/Archduchess_Elisabeth_of_Austria_(1922%E2%80%931993)" title="Archduchess Elisabeth of Austria (1922–1993)">Elisabeth</a></b></td>
<td>31 May 1922</td>
<td>7 January 1993<span style="display:none">(1993-01-07)</span> (aged&#160;70)</td>
<td>married (1949) Prince Heinrich Karl Vincenz <a href="/wiki/Princely_Family_of_Liechtenstein" class="mw-redirect" title="Princely Family of Liechtenstein">of Liechtenstein</a> (5 August 1916&#160;&#8211;&#32;17 April 1991), grandson of <a href="/wiki/Prince_Alfred_of_Liechtenstein" title="Prince Alfred of Liechtenstein">Prince Alfred</a>; five children.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<ul><li><span style="position: relative; top: 0.2em;"><sup><small>1</small></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/List_of_Polish_monarchs" title="List of Polish monarchs">Duke of Poland</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Piast_dynasty" title="Piast dynasty">Piast dynasty</a></li>
<li><span style="position: relative; top: 0.2em;"><sup><small>2</small></sup></span> During his reign obtained non-hereditary royal title</li>
